The following user(s) said Thank You: erfada , cuimingzhao Here and Now Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 04 Apr 2015 05:42 #231 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 I'm making transition from FT2 to real- time charts (demo). Very important thing I need to learn is not to miss trades that I am able to spot and execute flawlessly. When I miss perfect entry, and this happens quite often, I have urge to find another great setup as soon as possible. I don't look at situation objectivly, and spotted setup is not even nearly as good as the missed one. Solution to this problem is this: - be aware of this behaviour/emotion and get it under control - don't miss trades Everyday there is a trade with good RR (at least 5R) that I've seen many times already. It is just matter of letting go all mediocre setups and being ready to execute one or two trades a day when the best opportunity occurs. How to do that? I am in the process of putting into practice what I mentioned in my post #219. I reduced number of instruments I watch. Now it is 7 and in next week I will decide whether I schould increase or decrease this number. Every day after NY session I will review all trade opportunities (mostly LTF scalps) that occured that day. I need to be aware that I can let go even a few setups in a row, because soon there will be another great opportunity and it is worth to PATIENTLY wait for it. Every instrument I watch is analysed from Monthly to M1 timeframe, and I often go lower then that (ticks). Scalp opportunities may pop up very quickly on the market so I set alerts at important levels, and I update my analysis many times a day. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 03 Jul 2014 06:35 #62 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 One week ago I decided to finally figure out how IF draw these blue boxes and how to apply it into trading: www.forexfactory.com/attachment.php?atta...1423315&d=1399633037 After intense price reading sessions I had yesterday few eureka moments. My price reading skills definitely improved. These boxes can be drawn on any TF and they are really great tool. What they show is nothing new but it helped me to see price in different way - as zones. Edges of this zones are flag limits / FTRs. After price break flag limit/FTR new one is created. FO from this zone is often great QM lvl. I did analysis of NZDUSD. I tried to get into mind of ITs, I used some boxes and concepts I learned before. Your journal has been refreshing to follow. I wish you the best of success. Perhaps start out with micro lots, a good trade management plan (you stick to as if your life depends on it) and see how you react. I know what it is like to trade real money . Each person is different I suppose. At least for me, trading real money introduced me to myself very quickly, and I did not like the person I met. Like many poor newbs, I was completely oblivious to the realities of trading, and had my butt handed to me on more than one occasion. I often found myself giving back any and all profits, and then moving into revenge mode only to sustain even larger losses. I was probably part of the stupidest crowd of traders there were. Had money, but no plan, no experience - basically all the makings of someone bound for the 95% group of sunken traders. Thankfully, through much pain, reading and perseverance, I started to see the possibility that I could actually succeed as a trader. However, I was still lacking something. I just knew it! But I could not quite put my finger on it. To make a long story short, it was a place like this that I was lacking. I have found no other place that helps a guy/gal really make sense of what is going on in price. Seiden is great and does a nice job, but I love the hands we are in here at RTM. Much success to you, Michal. P.S. I recently started reading "Trading in the Zone" by M. Douglas. Interesting read on the psychological side of things in trading. On nasdaq there was nice FTR ( 24 Jul), but as we know price can make FO above FTR and then reverse. Go to your charts and check M1 above FTR in order to see where price bounced from. There is very nice minor FTR, great place for FO. How could we know if price is going to bounce there? Well, there was no engulf before price reached this level, but compression on approach told us what IT's intentions are. So it was very low risk trade with TG1 on next FTR below. If LL reached extreme orders then I put SL below demand. If not - I put SL below FL, because price may reach extreme orders in FL. Exit scenarios. I'll watch PA on approach to blue boxes. 1. 2. If IT's want to reverse price we will see CP on approach or there will be a flag formed below supply. I'm not going to exit before limit of this flag is engulfed. Difficult thing may be exiting on retrace - not instantly after engulf. That is one reason why I going to MASTER this setup and see how often I can exit on retrace. We know that when there is engulf of FL then price can retrace even beyond opposite FL. I'm going to find many examples of this scenario and see how deep this retrace can be, when price does a FO and where FO stops. FO can be anticipated when we have fresh level/ DP to engulf below FL. I wouldn't set a PO in this place especially if it is mid-range setup like this one. What is more important than RR is that in this way you increase probability. AUDUSD trade was at extreme, and offered really good RR. DP that was located on edge of CP was in very good place - just above recent highs - perfect for FO, and on RS flip so precise reaction at this level could be expected. I use only ForexTester. I'm still disovering new things - there is no point to trade yet. I want to go live and I'm excited when I think about it but I try to use it to motivate myself - the faster I have market worked out completly, the faster I can go live. My results will be fantastic. I'm also curious what will happen. The result is that I spent too much time looking on the same charts over and over again. I use my platform only for analysis on setups that already happened. But I practice trading. Only in ForexTester. It saves so much time. Using FT2 is strictly connected with rule number 2. 2. I save all trades from FT2, all observations and all conclusions I have while analysing charts. I make screens all the time and I read them on a regular basis. Sometimes I have 10 screenshots with notes for one trade from FT2. Its very time-consuming. But it is worth it. If I'm not sure about something (for example: can price go that far after ENG?) Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 06 Jan 2015 05:01 #195 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 Hi guys. naascott There are few things I want to see when I'm looking for good DP: - it can be located on swap zone - FO zone can be created below it ( short scenario) - also I like to see CP above strong DP - if price is compressed above, then it is more likely that price will be reversed on this DP - it is only fresh DP in the area DP is a barrier to ITs, because another sellers are waiting on this level (short scenario), so they need to do their manipulations before price reaches it - they need to create LQ and when they do that we see reversal patterns, FO zones etc. bobyv87 FL and PAZ between them work in every TF, therefore some FLs are more important than others, smaller or bigger FLs. There are diffrent cycles. It's quite difficult to learn spot all of this correctly but with a lot of practice it will become clear. And don't forget to look left: Markers are usually small FLs - ITs use them to reverse price before DP. I like to see when price bouces from this level few times - it creates FO zone below DP which provides LQ for ITs. I enter on FO to DP after confirmation - in this case it was eng into CPPAZ on approach. I thik that after this reversal PA SL can be above DP (grey box - because it is FL in itself - you can see this on m1 chart). But if situation is not very clear then you may use wider SL. The following user(s) said Thank You: erfada Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 13 Jan 2015 03:46 #204 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 Sorry for long reply time guys, I have been really busy lately. bobyv87 To define cycles you have to evaluate importance of FL. I'm not an expert at this yet, because I still have some things to learn about PAZ, but with practice I am more and more often able to spot this correctly on chart. I find it difficult to explain though. You have to think about space between FL's. When you find space that is limited from both sides by FL's, then after eng you expect price to go to next FL in direction of eng. Befere this happens price creates Fls as it retraces, but these FLs don't affect main move. They work in different cycles. Blue box that you marked is not FTR. It is reaction to DP on QM lvl. It is high that didn't engulf anything. Price engulfed FL at 3 and FL was made at 4. smimon I learned all of this from free RTM, and by doing price reading on historic data. I don't trade when I see only FO zone - it is not enough. FO zone is most of the times just a part of good setup. I can distinguish 2 types of setups with FO zones: - First one is setup where there is already a FO zone created before price arrives at particular lvl. For example there is nice fresh FTR and sing high / swing higs below it ( short scenario ). You definitely need confirmation PA in setup like this one on approach and reaction. - Second one is creating LQ after price shown reversal signals (ENG). I'll write more about my rules and trading plan soon. Befere I left there was a question about importance of FTRs. This is something that can not be answered in one post. The best way to learn this is to collect all great posts about FTRs/FLs, study them, and then practice. Mark all FL in different TFs and see how they interact with each other. When you don't know why something happend, write question on the chart, make some assumptions. Then go to then next example and do the same thing. There is finite number of "setups" in the market ( olny shapes may be a bit different) so sooner or later you'll find answers to your questions. Here is my RTM folder. I learn about FLs using method I described above: Today I took this trade on GOLD. Entered a bit too late, it was possible to get better RR from this: I recently started to watch TFs lower than M1 because there is a lot of great scalp opportunities that can not be seen so easily on m1, and sometimes it is impossible to mark all FLs precisely on m1. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 17 Mar 2015 07:52 #219 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 This folder contains everything related to trading, but majority of charts is FL analysis. Number of folders is so big, because I have many setups saved in separate folders, and I make new folder for every session on FT2 I realized recently that I'm pretty bad at decision making, I'm not patient enough on live charts, and I tend to make the same mistakes many times due to loose analysis. So I set some rules regarding my analysis during practise on FT2: I test one pair at a time and I have 3 separate charts. One is for general HTF view and I mark most important lvls and FLs on it. Second chart is for LTF FL analysis - sometimes it gets messy and that is why I need more than one chart. On third chart I mark S/R flips, swaps lvls - it is easy to miss them when chart is not neat and clean. Doing this I'm sure that I used propely my best analysis tools, and I wont do mistakes that could easily been avoided. And this is how I'm going to improve my decision making: - I take screenshots with my analysis and mark best places to look for PA - as price is approaching my lvl, I make decision - do I wait for confirmation or can I TT? why? I write this on screenshot. Decision is made. - let's say that I took TT. At this point I have marked on chart different sized FL that were formed on approach - I decide where is my TP1 and where are potential trouble areas - I make screen after TP1 , and then after every continuation ENG, or reversal ENG. - reversal ENG - I close my trade - continuation ENG - I make decision - where I'll let price retrace to, before closing my trade? where in next FL? As price is moving away (or towards) my entry I mark new FLs that are created, I mark CP, and strong SD lvls / ignored SD lvls, using always the same colors. I don't want to miss/ forget anything. Also I do this to develop habists: strong SD lvls formed against me ----> look for exit CP between my entry, and current price ----> I move my SL above last fresh SD lvl FL not engulfed --> exit etc. I know that this is basic stuff, but sometimes I ignored important information, because I wanted my FL analysis to be correct every single time. Its stupid, I know, but I have done this in the past on live account Every trade is narrated, and I also try to think like IT - what they want to accomplish, where is LQ for them - what are doing trend followers? - what are doing BO traders? - what are doing savy SD traders? In the next month I will continue practising in this way, and what is most important - I also want to apply this method to live charts. In this way I will have everything propely analysed, and decisions will be made only once - I wont stay undecided at any time. Analysis is made ---> its time to make decisions and write it down. New piece of PA created --> make analysis marking FLs, SD, CPs ---> make decision, just like on FT2 I described briefly how my perfect price reading session looks like. Probably I will post here example of this on real charts soon. It is easy to just fast-forward charts not thinking much about it. Its tempting as well - because there is a part of me that enjoys gambling. What I want to achieve in my every single price reading session is called "deliberate practice" - it takes big ammount of effort, but I believe that in this way I can get great results. I decided to start a new journal here, but this time it will be more results oriented. I'm going to post my statements regularly regardless of how good/bad it is. The reason for that is that I want to be honest about my performance in order to not create unrealistic expectations for new members - that can happen when winning trades are posted (mostly) and great setups explained. It all looks logical and easy in hindsight but even though I have a good understanding, in real time I have doubts all the time and it is difficult to make a good decision. So I'm going to talk about ways/techniques I will use to improve my performance and whether it worked or not, and if I managed to apply it correctly. I am also going to post a lot of setups with detailed explanations and commentary, as I used to in my old journal. Here goes my first statement. I started trading this account 25th of January - it is demo, because there is a lot of things I can improve, and losing a lot of real money had a really bad effect on my psyche and I had to take a break from trading... Trading live is no time for improvements - all bad habits can be changed while trading on demo and that is what I intend to do. I will continue this until I can see consistent results on weekly basis for the period of 2 months at least. The number of trades is much higher than a number of setups I traded. It is something I need to work on - I enjoy too much pressing these buttons. Also, there is a ton of breakeven/small loss/small profit trades - this is where I make an impulsive decision, or bad decision and realize it - then I close trades to avoid further losses. Overtrading is something I struggle with for a long time now, also respecting trading rules etc. I don't know if there is a hope in trading career for people like me, but I'm not going to give up... I think that I slightly improve over time even though I'm nowhere near to overcoming all psychological challenges of trading. The current result may seem pretty decent right now but the real challenge will come when I'll have next losing streak. I had one already - I survived, but suffered 10% drawdown. It was basically 2 setups where I entered too many times breaking MM rules. Another big issue is entering after a reaction to a level - RR is worse and bounce already happened - pointless. I'll work on that as well and I'll post updates on my progress in this journal. Where do I look for trades? What I aim to do is to take touch trades in the direction of the trend. But by trend, I mean situation when the price is coming from a valid zone and didn't reach the target yet. I basically try to find places for a touch trade after continuation engulfs. I do this when ITs have positions open and add on retraces by stopping traders out. When I see that price is reacting to valid target and there is some kind of reversal I assume ITs are short (for example) and then I look for opportunities to join the move. So even though most of my trades can be classified as continuation trades, all of them are reversal trades at the same time - because if I sell, I sell against strong (usually) bullish move on smaller scale, and vice versa. It is important to analyze all scales and to make sure that you are not trading against direction signaled by engulf on a higher scale. I'll quickly mark EUR/USD charts to show what I mean: This quasimodo is beautiful you can see how clever ITs are. Look how long price was going up. As price moves up more and more people are biased long and then big push happens. The way I think about this is as follows: ITs don't have to do much here. People are biased long and ITs "add fuel to the fire" to make the move look strong/scary/exciting, whatever. The point is that now price is where they want to sell at and people keep buying this thinking that price will go up forever . Btw. there is a big daily "marubozu" candle after pin bar - some people consider this an excellent buy signal. Countertrend moves are usually strong and it may be counter-intuitive to trade against but that is what we do. Here is nice examples of moves like that. I marked them with arrows. Red lines show where people got stopped out recently. So I think that ITs are still short and there is potential for continuation as there is a lot of space below. Of course, it is easy to mark this stuff in hindsight - much harder to do it real time because it often seems that move is genuine and it will stop you out, but you need to pay attention to bigger picture and trade against moves like these fearlessly. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 2.0 13 Feb 2017 07:20 #5 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 I have currently one trade open on eurusd, that I didn't explain in my last post. It is a nice example of continuation trade. I noticed some time ago that there are a lot of continuation opportunities after breaking QM levels. And there is a good reason for that so this setup gives me a nice opportunity to elaborate on that. I try to look at price more in terms of orders opened by ITs. So at this point, we can say that there was a quasimodo, and then more shorts were opened on the way down (see my last post). The idea for continuation or at least bounce from broken QM lvl seems logical to me. Because I ask myself this - what needs to happen for QM lvl to be broken? We know that people get trapped and orders remain at the level - in this case, buy orders. If ITs want to push the price down they need to sell against buy orders. So they have shorts orders open at the level. If price goes above this price level then it means that they will get into drawdown on these positions. They don't want drawdown, so they will protect this level by adding shorts and pushing price down. There are also other things to consider, for example - had ITs have a chance to reverse positions already? or is there a better place for retrace? You can see on my chart that there is no obvious better place for retrace (CP). This eurusd chart is beautiful, setups are rarely this clean. Its still possible that I am wrong of course. But thinking about orders in this way will allow for great trades when your read is correct. So what I learned is that compression is a great indicator of where you can expect short retrace. Sometimes price moves from a target area, and didn't reach the opposite target, and there is "no space" for deep retraces - so you can get in on the move on short RT. The following user(s) said Thank You: Lupy , Ywicked , moogroo , Al3cs , staurum Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 2.0 19 Feb 2017 23:01 #7 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 I took a lot of trades this week, too much to post in real time so I'm going to post them in bulk. As I go forward with this journal I'll work on reducing the number of entries and posting them more often. I unintentionally developed a habit of entering many times with small lots instead of picking one entry point. There are a lot of reasons for that, for example: - entering before FL (fear of missing out), and then adding more when the price gets closer to MPL - being afraid to enter with full risk before price reacts to a level and. Seeing price bounce gives me a feeling of being correct, so I enter. But this feeling is not really based on my knowledge. We need to anticipate and not react to what price is doing. So the best moment to buy is before any signs of price rising occur, and same goes for shorts. I think that most of traders experience this - when the price is moving up it feels like it will countinue, and vice versa. Sometimes even direction of the last M1 candle influences how I feel about my trade. I need to learn to ignore this feeling. - not being prepared for entry beforehand. When this happens I see price reacting to something, then I do analysis, I find out that price is reacting to a MPL of some important level and then I try to enter I think that using only pending orders is the way to go. Using pending orders means that I was prepared for entry and I anticipate what will happen, and I was completely prepared with my analysis beforehand instead of reacting to what price is currently doing . Here is my statement. This week was a fail, biggest drawdown so far. But it shed some light at biggest mistakes that I make in trading. Last week only: So, as you can see at the beginning of the week I had a lot of winners and I took partial profits regularly. But then after a couple of losers instead of trading the bounces, I tried to catch swings and holding trades until it hits final TP to make up for losses. Trying to catch a swing is probably the biggest mistakes I make in trading. And it happens because I try to recover from losses. Of course, it doesn't happen and I only lose more. This happens when I am strongly convinced that price didn't hit the TG yet, and I am often right, but the problem with catching swings is that: - I ignore good entry points - because I don't want to miss the obvious move - I don't take partial profits - I move my stops because there is still valid engulf signal that didn't play out yet or I enter soon after being stopped out - I don't wait for deepest possible RT This big drawdown is the result of doing that on two correlated pairs at the same time, and this was a break of MM rules. The red circle is where I took the biggest loss - I not only moved my SL but also added at the next level. Around the same time, I was trying to catch a move on GU, and I lost a lot. I am still convicted that EU and GU will go down, I will write why in my next post with the trades that I have currently open. It was okay to short EU, but I did it incorrectly. I didn't wait for best prices with my entries and I didn't take partial TPs. Look at these left shoulder levels, and how embarrassing my trades look now: The correct way to trade is to take touch trades in direction of the trend, and the trend is down on EU (because it is coming from a valid level and TG for shorts is not hit yet). Entry from PO, then TP1 after the bounce at 1-2R, so that after SL overall result is small profit or BE. So the goal for next week is put more time into analyzing charts in the evening and in the morning and finding places where I can set POs. I'll enter only from POs and take TP1 after the bounce. If I failed to take TP1 after the bounce, I'll close my trade at BE. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 2.0 26 Feb 2017 13:40 #13 staurum --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 940 Thank you received: 1954 I kinda agree with Edge. Noticed that i think FTR concept has "shifted" a little. If the question is whether or not any of the boxes is FTR, then we gotta zoom in LTF to see where the break occurs and the deepest retrace point, and the whole zone would be our FTR box. Up until, it reaches the target of the break on the left, which should be another FL on the same scale; then the FTR box is our FL. If the question is whether or not this box can be trade-able, I would wait until the break reach a target so activate the box as "FL", then we will have a clear PAZ to trade. The following user(s) said Thank You: erfada , cuimingzhao Here and Now Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 04 Apr 2015 05:42 #231 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 I'm making transition from FT2 to real- time charts (demo). Very important thing I need to learn is not to miss trades that I am able to spot and execute flawlessly. When I miss perfect entry, and this happens quite often, I have urge to find another great setup as soon as possible. I don't look at situation objectivly, and spotted setup is not even nearly as good as the missed one. Solution to this problem is this: - be aware of this behaviour/emotion and get it under control - don't miss trades Everyday there is a trade with good RR (at least 5R) that I've seen many times already. It is just matter of letting go all mediocre setups and being ready to execute one or two trades a day when the best opportunity occurs. How to do that? I am in the process of putting into practice what I mentioned in my post #219. I reduced number of instruments I watch. Now it is 7 and in next week I will decide whether I schould increase or decrease this number. Every day after NY session I will review all trade opportunities (mostly LTF scalps) that occured that day. I need to be aware that I can let go even a few setups in a row, because soon there will be another great opportunity and it is worth to PATIENTLY wait for it. Every instrument I watch is analysed from Monthly to M1 timeframe, and I often go lower then that (ticks). Scalp opportunities may pop up very quickly on the market so I set alerts at important levels, and I update my analysis many times a day. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 03 Jul 2014 06:35 #62 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 One week ago I decided to finally figure out how IF draw these blue boxes and how to apply it into trading: www.forexfactory.com/attachment.php?atta...1423315&d=1399633037 After intense price reading sessions I had yesterday few eureka moments. My price reading skills definitely improved. These boxes can be drawn on any TF and they are really great tool. What they show is nothing new but it helped me to see price in different way - as zones. Edges of this zones are flag limits / FTRs. After price break flag limit/FTR new one is created. FO from this zone is often great QM lvl. I did analysis of NZDUSD. I tried to get into mind of ITs, I used some boxes and concepts I learned before. Your journal has been refreshing to follow. I wish you the best of success. Perhaps start out with micro lots, a good trade management plan (you stick to as if your life depends on it) and see how you react. I know what it is like to trade real money . Each person is different I suppose. At least for me, trading real money introduced me to myself very quickly, and I did not like the person I met. Like many poor newbs, I was completely oblivious to the realities of trading, and had my butt handed to me on more than one occasion. I often found myself giving back any and all profits, and then moving into revenge mode only to sustain even larger losses. I was probably part of the stupidest crowd of traders there were. Had money, but no plan, no experience - basically all the makings of someone bound for the 95% group of sunken traders. Thankfully, through much pain, reading and perseverance, I started to see the possibility that I could actually succeed as a trader. However, I was still lacking something. I just knew it! But I could not quite put my finger on it. To make a long story short, it was a place like this that I was lacking. I have found no other place that helps a guy/gal really make sense of what is going on in price. Seiden is great and does a nice job, but I love the hands we are in here at RTM. Much success to you, Michal. P.S. I recently started reading "Trading in the Zone" by M. Douglas. Interesting read on the psychological side of things in trading. The following user(s) said Thank You: kkb Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 17 Aug 2014 02:51 #127 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 It's all about FTR's. Most recent peak doesn't tell you what is going on you need to mark FTR's within it, and check what it is reacting to. On nasdaq there was nice FTR ( 24 Jul), but as we know price can make FO above FTR and then reverse. Go to your charts and check M1 above FTR in order to see where price bounced from. There is very nice minor FTR, great place for FO. How could we know if price is going to bounce there? Well, there was no engulf before price reached this level, but compression on approach told us what IT's intentions are. So it was very low risk trade with TG1 on next FTR below. I'll have a check list that will help me to stick to my plan. I'll master each of this setup and I'll ONLY trade what I know that works and I know how to manage it. I want to improve win ratio and, most of all be able to exit my trade without any doubt when reaction is not good enough. Emotional attachment to trades is really terrible thing. Also I give up temporarily extra tight SL trades. I want to know that I'm able to make consistent profits, and having much more winers than losers is my goal now. Even if they are small account will grow. In my price reading I'm going to analyse every turn in the market - if I find good zone that didn't work I'll always analyse it in M1 detail on approach and reaction. By now I more focused on zones that worked well - and this is a reason why I have hard time to exit when it is not too late - with smaller loss than my SL , BE, or even small profit. Here is setup 1 (perfect example): It is basicly a QM settup. What I want to highlight here is clear FL and engulf of it after reaction to nice demand. If LL reached extreme orders then I put SL below demand. If not - I put SL below FL, because price may reach extreme orders in FL. Exit scenarios. I'll watch PA on approach to blue boxes. 1. 2. If IT's want to reverse price we will see CP on approach or there will be a flag formed below supply. I'm not going to exit before limit of this flag is engulfed. Difficult thing may be exiting on retrace - not instantly after engulf. That is one reason why I going to MASTER this setup and see how often I can exit on retrace. We know that when there is engulf of FL then price can retrace even beyond opposite FL. I'm going to find many examples of this scenario and see how deep this retrace can be, when price does a FO and where FO stops. FO can be anticipated when we have fresh level/ DP to engulf below FL. Here is one example I found on EURAUD: Another example is in my post #150. I'll spend some time mastering this setup. There can be really many variables of it. It is one setup but once mastered there will be many opportunities to profit from it. What I showed above is QM setup it can be also a diamond, can can. I've put it in context and described briefly how to manage it. I also have better idea on elements I must work on. For example - continuation engulf showed in last diagram. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 20 Nov 2014 01:11 #182 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 Few weeks ago I changed the way I learn. I've set 2 rules for myself, and I want to share it with you guys, because since I'm doing it I'm learning faster than ever. 1. I'm not trading live and demo untill I'm one with the market. I use only ForexTester. I'm still disovering new things - there is no point to trade yet. I want to go live and I'm excited when I think about it but I try to use it to motivate myself - the faster I have market worked out completly, the faster I can go live. My results will be fantastic. Trading will be stress - free. Why I'm not trading demo? Because I care about my trades. When I set pending orders I have to watch approach & reaction and look for reversal PA. I'm also curious what will happen. The result is that I spent too much time looking on the same charts over and over again. I use my platform only for analysis on setups that already happened. But I practice trading. Only in ForexTester. It saves so much time. Using FT2 is strictly connected with rule number 2. 2. I save all trades from FT2, all observations and all conclusions I have while analysing charts. I make screens all the time and I read them on a regular basis. Sometimes I have 10 screenshots with notes for one trade from FT2. Its very time-consuming. But it is worth it. If I'm not sure about something (for example: can price go that far after ENG?) I write question on a screen. I have many of those questions and I'm finding answer for each of this questions after analysing many similar setups. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 06 Jan 2015 05:01 #195 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 Hi guys. naascott There are few things I want to see when I'm looking for good DP: - it can be located on swap zone - FO zone can be created below it ( short scenario) - also I like to see CP above strong DP - if price is compressed above, then it is more likely that price will be reversed on this DP - it is only fresh DP in the area DP is a barrier to ITs, because another sellers are waiting on this level (short scenario), so they need to do their manipulations before price reaches it - they need to create LQ and when they do that we see reversal patterns, FO zones etc. bobyv87 FL and PAZ between them work in every TF, therefore some FLs are more important than others, smaller or bigger FLs. There are diffrent cycles. It's quite difficult to learn spot all of this correctly but with a lot of practice it will become clear. And don't forget to look left: Markers are usually small FLs - ITs use them to reverse price before DP. I like to see when price bouces from this level few times - it creates FO zone below DP which provides LQ for ITs. I enter on FO to DP after confirmation - in this case it was eng into CPPAZ on approach. I thik that after this reversal PA SL can be above DP (grey box - because it is FL in itself - you can see this on m1 chart). But if situation is not very clear then you may use wider SL. Michal238 wrote: Hi guys. naascott There are few things I want to see when I'm looking for good DP: - it can be located on swap zone - FO zone can be created below it ( short scenario) - also I like to see CP above strong DP - if price is compressed above, then it is more likely that price will be reversed on this DP - it is only fresh DP in the area DP is a barrier to ITs, because another sellers are waiting on this level (short scenario), so they need to do their manipulations before price reaches it - they need to create LQ and when they do that we see reversal patterns, FO zones etc. bobyv87 FL and PAZ between them work in every TF, therefore some FLs are more important than others, smaller or bigger FLs. There are diffrent cycles. It's quite difficult to learn spot all of this correctly but with a lot of practice it will become clear. And don't forget to look left: Markers are usually small FLs - ITs use them to reverse price before DP. I like to see when price bouces from this level few times - it creates FO zone below DP which provides LQ for ITs. I enter on FO to DP after confirmation - in this case it was eng into CPPAZ on approach. I thik that after this reversal PA SL can be above DP (grey box - because it is FL in itself - you can see this on m1 chart). But if situation is not very clear then you may use wider SL. The following user(s) said Thank You: erfada Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 13 Jan 2015 03:46 #204 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 Sorry for long reply time guys, I have been really busy lately. bobyv87 To define cycles you have to evaluate importance of FL. I'm not an expert at this yet, because I still have some things to learn about PAZ, but with practice I am more and more often able to spot this correctly on chart. I find it difficult to explain though. You have to think about space between FL's. When you find space that is limited from both sides by FL's, then after eng you expect price to go to next FL in direction of eng. Befere this happens price creates Fls as it retraces, but these FLs don't affect main move. They work in different cycles. Blue box that you marked is not FTR. It is reaction to DP on QM lvl. It is high that didn't engulf anything. Price engulfed FL at 3 and FL was made at 4. smimon I learned all of this from free RTM, and by doing price reading on historic data. The following user(s) said Thank You: Michal238 , erfada Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 23 Jan 2015 00:03 #210 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 bobyv I started to notice FO zones when I tried to think about ITs and how they need to create LQ by hunting for stops and making BO traders open wrong positions. I don't trade when I see only FO zone - it is not enough. FO zone is most of the times just a part of good setup. I can distinguish 2 types of setups with FO zones: - First one is setup where there is already a FO zone created before price arrives at particular lvl. For example there is nice fresh FTR and sing high / swing higs below it ( short scenario ). You definitely need confirmation PA in setup like this one on approach and reaction. - Second one is creating LQ after price shown reversal signals (ENG). This setup combines these 2 types: Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. I have long list of rules and I'm polishing my trading plan. In next few weeks, I'll trade on demo account (also FT2) focusing on taking confirmed entries that fit into my trading plan. I'll write more about my rules and trading plan soon. Befere I left there was a question about importance of FTRs. This is something that can not be answered in one post. The best way to learn this is to collect all great posts about FTRs/FLs, study them, and then practice. Mark all FL in different TFs and see how they interact with each other. When you don't know why something happend, write question on the chart, make some assumptions. Then go to then next example and do the same thing. There is finite number of "setups" in the market ( olny shapes may be a bit different) so sooner or later you'll find answers to your questions. Here is my RTM folder. I learn about FLs using method I described above: Today I took this trade on GOLD. Entered a bit too late, it was possible to get better RR from this: I recently started to watch TFs lower than M1 because there is a lot of great scalp opportunities that can not be seen so easily on m1, and sometimes it is impossible to mark all FLs precisely on m1. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 17 Mar 2015 07:52 #219 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 This folder contains everything related to trading, but majority of charts is FL analysis. Number of folders is so big, because I have many setups saved in separate folders, and I make new folder for every session on FT2 I realized recently that I'm pretty bad at decision making, I'm not patient enough on live charts, and I tend to make the same mistakes many times due to loose analysis. So I set some rules regarding my analysis during practise on FT2: I test one pair at a time and I have 3 separate charts. One is for general HTF view and I mark most important lvls and FLs on it. Second chart is for LTF FL analysis - sometimes it gets messy and that is why I need more than one chart. On third chart I mark S/R flips, swaps lvls - it is easy to miss them when chart is not neat and clean. Doing this I'm sure that I used propely my best analysis tools, and I wont do mistakes that could easily been avoided. And this is how I'm going to improve my decision making: - I take screenshots with my analysis and mark best places to look for PA - as price is approaching my lvl, I make decision - do I wait for confirmation or can I TT? why? I write this on screenshot. Decision is made. - let's say that I took TT. At this point I have marked on chart different sized FL that were formed on approach - I decide where is my TP1 and where are potential trouble areas - I make screen after TP1 , and then after every continuation ENG, or reversal ENG. - reversal ENG - I close my trade - continuation ENG - I make decision - where I'll let price retrace to, before closing my trade? where in next FL? As price is moving away (or towards) my entry I mark new FLs that are created, I mark CP, and strong SD lvls / ignored SD lvls, using always the same colors. I don't want to miss/ forget anything. Also I do this to develop habists: strong SD lvls formed against me ----> look for exit CP between my entry, and current price ----> I move my SL above last fresh SD lvl FL not engulfed --> exit etc. I know that this is basic stuff, but sometimes I ignored important information, because I wanted my FL analysis to be correct every single time. Its stupid, I know, but I have done this in the past on live account Every trade is narrated, and I also try to think like IT - what they want to accomplish, where is LQ for them - what are doing trend followers? - what are doing BO traders? - what are doing savy SD traders? In the next month I will continue practising in this way, and what is most important - I also want to apply this method to live charts. In this way I will have everything propely analysed, and decisions will be made only once - I wont stay undecided at any time. Analysis is made ---> its time to make decisions and write it down. New piece of PA created --> make analysis marking FLs, SD, CPs ---> make decision, just like on FT2 I described briefly how my perfect price reading session looks like. Probably I will post here example of this on real charts soon. It is easy to just fast-forward charts not thinking much about it. Its tempting as well - because there is a part of me that enjoys gambling. What I want to achieve in my every single price reading session is called "deliberate practice" - it takes big ammount of effort, but I believe that in this way I can get great results. Category not found Michal's Journal 2.0 - RTM Community ReadTheMarket.com The only School which understands The Market Index Recent Topics New Topic No Replies My Topics Private Messages Preferences Welcome, indo-investasi Last login: Today 01:42 Forum Members' Journals Journals Michal's Journal 2.0 Reply Topic Favorite Page: 1 TOPIC: Michal's Journal 2.0 Michal's Journal 2.0 11 Feb 2017 06:40 #1 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 Hi everyone I'm happy to be back and thank you IF for putting me on the list. I decided to start a new journal here, but this time it will be more results oriented. I'm going to post my statements regularly regardless of how good/bad it is. The reason for that is that I want to be honest about my performance in order to not create unrealistic expectations for new members - that can happen when winning trades are posted (mostly) and great setups explained. It all looks logical and easy in hindsight but even though I have a good understanding, in real time I have doubts all the time and it is difficult to make a good decision. So I'm going to talk about ways/techniques I will use to improve my performance and whether it worked or not, and if I managed to apply it correctly. I am also going to post a lot of setups with detailed explanations and commentary, as I used to in my old journal. Here goes my first statement. I started trading this account 25th of January - it is demo, because there is a lot of things I can improve, and losing a lot of real money had a really bad effect on my psyche and I had to take a break from trading... Trading live is no time for improvements - all bad habits can be changed while trading on demo and that is what I intend to do. I will continue this until I can see consistent results on weekly basis for the period of 2 months at least. The number of trades is much higher than a number of setups I traded. It is something I need to work on - I enjoy too much pressing these buttons. Also, there is a ton of breakeven/small loss/small profit trades - this is where I make an impulsive decision, or bad decision and realize it - then I close trades to avoid further losses. Overtrading is something I struggle with for a long time now, also respecting trading rules etc. I don't know if there is a hope in trading career for people like me, but I'm not going to give up... I think that I slightly improve over time even though I'm nowhere near to overcoming all psychological challenges of trading. The current result may seem pretty decent right now but the real challenge will come when I'll have next losing streak. I had one already - I survived, but suffered 10% drawdown. It was basically 2 setups where I entered too many times breaking MM rules. Another big issue is entering after a reaction to a level - RR is worse and bounce already happened - pointless. I'll work on that as well and I'll post updates on my progress in this journal. Where do I look for trades? What I aim to do is to take touch trades in the direction of the trend. But by trend, I mean situation when the price is coming from a valid zone and didn't reach the target yet. I basically try to find places for a touch trade after continuation engulfs. I do this when ITs have positions open and add on retraces by stopping traders out. When I see that price is reacting to valid target and there is some kind of reversal I assume ITs are short (for example) and then I look for opportunities to join the move. So even though most of my trades can be classified as continuation trades, all of them are reversal trades at the same time - because if I sell, I sell against strong (usually) bullish move on smaller scale, and vice versa. It is important to analyze all scales and to make sure that you are not trading against direction signaled by engulf on a higher scale. I'll quickly mark EUR/USD charts to show what I mean: This quasimodo is beautiful you can see how clever ITs are. Look how long price was going up. As price moves up more and more people are biased long and then big push happens. The way I think about this is as follows: ITs don't have to do much here. People are biased long and ITs "add fuel to the fire" to make the move look strong/scary/exciting, whatever. The point is that now price is where they want to sell at and people keep buying this thinking that price will go up forever . Btw. there is a big daily "marubozu" candle after pin bar - some people consider this an excellent buy signal. Countertrend moves are usually strong and it may be counter-intuitive to trade against but that is what we do. Here is nice examples of moves like that. I marked them with arrows. Red lines show where people got stopped out recently. So I think that ITs are still short and there is potential for continuation as there is a lot of space below. Of course, it is easy to mark this stuff in hindsight - much harder to do it real time because it often seems that move is genuine and it will stop you out, but you need to pay attention to bigger picture and trade against moves like these fearlessly. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 2.0 13 Feb 2017 07:20 #5 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 I have currently one trade open on eurusd, that I didn't explain in my last post. It is a nice example of continuation trade. I noticed some time ago that there are a lot of continuation opportunities after breaking QM levels. And there is a good reason for that so this setup gives me a nice opportunity to elaborate on that. I try to look at price more in terms of orders opened by ITs. So at this point, we can say that there was a quasimodo, and then more shorts were opened on the way down (see my last post). The idea for continuation or at least bounce from broken QM lvl seems logical to me. Because I ask myself this - what needs to happen for QM lvl to be broken? We know that people get trapped and orders remain at the level - in this case, buy orders. If ITs want to push the price down they need to sell against buy orders. So they have shorts orders open at the level. If price goes above this price level then it means that they will get into drawdown on these positions. They don't want drawdown, so they will protect this level by adding shorts and pushing price down. There are also other things to consider, for example - had ITs have a chance to reverse positions already? or is there a better place for retrace? You can see on my chart that there is no obvious better place for retrace (CP). This eurusd chart is beautiful, setups are rarely this clean. Its still possible that I am wrong of course. But thinking about orders in this way will allow for great trades when your read is correct. So what I learned is that compression is a great indicator of where you can expect short retrace. Sometimes price moves from a target area, and didn't reach the opposite target, and there is "no space" for deep retraces - so you can get in on the move on short RT. The following user(s) said Thank You: Lupy , Ywicked , moogroo , Al3cs , staurum Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 2.0 19 Feb 2017 23:01 #7 Michal238 Topic Author --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 176 Thank you received: 1027 I took a lot of trades this week, too much to post in real time so I'm going to post them in bulk. As I go forward with this journal I'll work on reducing the number of entries and posting them more often. I unintentionally developed a habit of entering many times with small lots instead of picking one entry point. There are a lot of reasons for that, for example: - entering before FL (fear of missing out), and then adding more when the price gets closer to MPL - being afraid to enter with full risk before price reacts to a level and. Seeing price bounce gives me a feeling of being correct, so I enter. But this feeling is not really based on my knowledge. We need to anticipate and not react to what price is doing. So the best moment to buy is before any signs of price rising occur, and same goes for shorts. I think that most of traders experience this - when the price is moving up it feels like it will countinue, and vice versa. Sometimes even direction of the last M1 candle influences how I feel about my trade. I need to learn to ignore this feeling. - not being prepared for entry beforehand. When this happens I see price reacting to something, then I do analysis, I find out that price is reacting to a MPL of some important level and then I try to enter I think that using only pending orders is the way to go. Using pending orders means that I was prepared for entry and I anticipate what will happen, and I was completely prepared with my analysis beforehand instead of reacting to what price is currently doing . Here is my statement. This week was a fail, biggest drawdown so far. But it shed some light at biggest mistakes that I make in trading. Last week only: So, as you can see at the beginning of the week I had a lot of winners and I took partial profits regularly. But then after a couple of losers instead of trading the bounces, I tried to catch swings and holding trades until it hits final TP to make up for losses. Trying to catch a swing is probably the biggest mistakes I make in trading. And it happens because I try to recover from losses. Of course, it doesn't happen and I only lose more. This happens when I am strongly convinced that price didn't hit the TG yet, and I am often right, but the problem with catching swings is that: - I ignore good entry points - because I don't want to miss the obvious move - I don't take partial profits - I move my stops because there is still valid engulf signal that didn't play out yet or I enter soon after being stopped out - I don't wait for deepest possible RT This big drawdown is the result of doing that on two correlated pairs at the same time, and this was a break of MM rules. The red circle is where I took the biggest loss - I not only moved my SL but also added at the next level. Around the same time, I was trying to catch a move on GU, and I lost a lot. I am still convicted that EU and GU will go down, I will write why in my next post with the trades that I have currently open. It was okay to short EU, but I did it incorrectly. I didn't wait for best prices with my entries and I didn't take partial TPs. Look at these left shoulder levels, and how embarrassing my trades look now: The correct way to trade is to take touch trades in direction of the trend, and the trend is down on EU (because it is coming from a valid level and TG for shorts is not hit yet). Entry from PO, then TP1 after the bounce at 1-2R, so that after SL overall result is small profit or BE. So the goal for next week is put more time into analyzing charts in the evening and in the morning and finding places where I can set POs. I'll enter only from POs and take TP1 after the bounce. If I failed to take TP1 after the bounce, I'll close my trade at BE. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Michal's Journal 2.0 26 Feb 2017 13:40 #13 staurum --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 940 Thank you received: 1954 I kinda agree with Edge. Noticed that i think FTR concept has "shifted" a little. If the question is whether or not any of the boxes is FTR, then we gotta zoom in LTF to see where the break occurs and the deepest retrace point, and the whole zone would be our FTR box. Up until, it reaches the target of the break on the left, which should be another FL on the same scale; then the FTR box is our FL. If the question is whether or not this box can be trade-able, I would wait until the break reach a target so activate the box as "FL", then we will have a clear PAZ to trade.
